# Global Strategy

# Quantamental Signal Ideas - Top & Bottom Ranked Stocks

Details on the methodology/model can be found in our Q-Series report

## 11 Quantamental signals to spot opportunities in global equities

We recently introduced our new quantamental framework for single stocks. This model uses 11 high-performing indicators that aim to extract information from price momentum, stocks’ fundamentals and investors’ sentiment/positioning... In this note, we flag stocks across five regions (US, EU, China, Japan and Australia) that screen well quantitatively in our framework and qualitatively based on UBS analysts’ ratings. An easy-to-read dashboard will help visualize the quantamental scores, while our analysts have shared a short blurb highlighting their fundamental views on the selected stocks.

## What are the model’s recommendations today?

Based on the stock-level scores and an optimisation for portfolio balance, we find that the highest-scoring stocks tend to come from sectors like Auto Components, Energy, Transportation, Capital Goods and Communication Services. The lowest-scoring stock list is dominated by Food Beverage & Tobacco, Household Personal Products, Staples, Health Care, Tech and Real Estate stocks. Do note these recommendations can differ from the fundamental views of our equity strategy team.

## What are the industries whose rankings have changed the most?

Compared to the previous update (done on 15th November), our model is raising its (already high) risk-on stance, reverting its year-end risk reduction. Most industries have seen their overall scores upgraded, especially the cyclical ones like Industrials, Financials and Real Estate. Defensives are exhibiting the lowest improvements, when Energy is the only sector where scores have deteriorated, albeit from a very high level.

## What are the notable differences across regions?

Staples stocks exhibit great scores in Japan but not in the other four regions. Real estate companies rate poorly in the US and China but great in Japan, Australia and Europe. Materials and Tech names continue to rank (relatively) badly in China. Utilities in Australia scores very low as well, especially compared to other markets.

UBS publishes a report that utilizes a quantamental framework for single stocks known as the "Quantamental Signals Strategy." The model uses 11 high-performing indicators to filter a trading signal for each stock globally. If you have any questions about this product, please speak to your UBS sales representative or email the Quantitative Research Team at qa@ubs.com. An easy-to-read dashboard that includes all stocks listed on the S&P 500, Eurostoxx 600, MSCI China, Nikkei 225 and S&P/ASX 200 is also available, and you should contact your UBS sales representative or the Quantitative Research team at qa@ubs.com for details and pricing.

## Quick reminder of the 11 quantamental signals

<table><tr><td>i)</td><td>(Four) CTA Momentum Signals: aim to benefit from the persistence in asset prices. Our CTA trend-following algorithm is</td></tr><tr><td>ii)</td><td>applied on 1) stock, 2) industry, 3) sector and 4) country index prices (Three) Regime Probability Signals: rely on the principle that assets&#x27; prices are greatly influenced by economic regimes.</td></tr><tr><td>iii)</td><td>Estimating each regime probability and positioning accordingly can be a great source of alpha Crowding Signal: empirical work suggests we should participate during the building phase of a &#x27;consensus&#x27; trade and</td></tr><tr><td>iv)</td><td>only be contrarian - i.e. play short-term mean reversion - when positioning has become extreme EPS Growth (ML vs Consensus) Signal: attempt to identify stocks with the highest risk of upgrades/downgrades from</td></tr><tr><td>v)</td><td>analysts. Surprises are powerful drivers of absolute and relative returns (Extreme) Rates Momentum Signal: speed and magnitude of bond moves are ones of the key factors of investors&#x27; risk</td></tr><tr><td>vi)</td><td>appetite. Increasing equity risk after a strong rally or sell-off in rates has historically been rewarded Risk Targeting Signal: risk targeting is an &#x27;optimized&#x27; long-only strategy that relies on two levers - volatilities and</td></tr><tr><td>vii)</td><td>correlations - to produce superior returns Up&amp;Down Volatility Signal: is a price-based indicator of positioning that gauges the probability of a trend reversal</td></tr><tr><td>viii)</td><td>Options Volumes Signal: is a leading indicator of investors&#x27; nervousness. Some investors seek to postpone the unpleasant feeling of crystallizing losses using derivatives hedging. More often than not, it leads to capitulation</td></tr><tr><td>ix)</td><td>Adjusted Forward PE Ratio Signal: value is a popular strategy for patient investors. We enhance one of the most popular valuation indictor, the fPE Ratio, by adjusting the numerator (i.e. prices) by the quality of the companies&#x27; balance sheets</td></tr><tr><td>x)</td><td>Sector PMI (New Orders) Signal: relies on the same economic priors as the Regime Probability signal. The main difference is that rather than a single leading index, we utilise sector level data on a specific leading indicator that surveys businesses directly on their forward looking business activity</td></tr><tr><td>xi)</td><td>Earnings Revisions Signal: profits from revisions in analysts&#x27; earnings estimates. Even small changes in expectations can have a big impact on a stock&#x27;s price</td></tr></table>

## What our US (fundamental) sector analysts think about our top/bottom picks?

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Communication Services

Lloyd Walmsley on Meta: Meta remains one of our top picks in the mega cap US Internet space, trading at just 15.6x our base case '25 GAAP EPS of \$22.70 and we think EPS consensus for '25 at \$17.54 looks low. In our view, the key underappreciated growth drivers are: (1) improving engagement across surfaces, (2) better ad tech including Advantage+, (3) ramping Reels monetization, (4) expanding advertiser base through new formats like Click-to-Message Ads, and (5) several nascent products like Shop Ads and Threads, all of which should support ongoing share gains in the online ad market. We also see Meta’s genAI chatbot monetization opportunity as significant and underappreciated. In the near term, these new genAI chatbots should bring more intent data to better target ads and over the midterm term could be monetized with search ads. Reiterate Buy and \$425 PT.

John Hodulik on Walt Disney: We have a Buy rating on the stock. Disney should see improving losses in its DTC segment and breakeven in 2H while profitability in Parks remains strong. We also believe the company is looking to establish partnerships with some of the larger sports leagues and tech companies ahead of the launch of the streaming version of ESPN. Renewed focus on its core franchises should help content performance exiting F24.

John Hodulik on Fox Corp: We have a Buy rating on Fox. The company is seeing pressure on advertising from lower ratings but affiliate trends remain solid and the company should benefit from the political cycle and upcoming Presidential elections in the US, driving mid-teens EBITDA growth in F25. With the best balance sheet in traditional media and \~\$4B in cash, the company can be opportunistic as the industry approaches the next round of consolidation.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Consumer Discretionary

Robin Farley on Royal Caribbean Cruises: RCL is one of our top picks for ‘24 as we see continued recovery in cruise travel and a price gap to land based that should help drive ticket price increases. RCL has a very successful Caribbean strategy and good expense discipline. We have a Buy rating.

Jay Sole on Ralph Lauren Corp: We have a Buy rating on RL. We think the market doesn't appreciate the transformational changes RL has made to its brand image, distribution model, and cost structure. We forecast a 12% 5-yr. EPS CAGR and believe the stock looks inexpensive at just 13.5x FY2 EPS (link).

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Consumer Discretionary

Lloyd Walmsley on Expedia Group Inc: We are Neutral rated on Expedia. The company is now trading at an 11% discount to BKNG on GAAP P/E, compared to the 22% discount in 4Q19. We lack confidence in the marketing strategy and margin implications as it expand its OneKey initiative, and the company continues to lose share in key markets and products.

Jay Sole on Tapestry Inc: We are Neutral on TPR. We believe the two key NTM stock price drivers will be 1) EPS trends for both TPR and CPRI; and 2) Deal synergy expectations. We see more downside than upside risk to the Street’s EPS outlook for TPR/CPRI due to soft macro trends and the risk we have underestimated how much it will cost TPR to fix CPRI’s brands. However, despite these downside risks, we continue to rate TPR Neutral. The main reason is we believe the stock’s 8x P/E adequately reflects these risks.

Chris Kuntarich on Etsy Inc: We enter into '24 with a neutral outlook on Etsy as shares trade at the low-end of the '22 range at 15x '24 EBITDA , but price in a return to MSD GMS growth in '25 (St +8% YoY). While we see the opportunity to drive cost savings and return capital faster, these upside drivers are offset by topline related risks in '24. With regards to topline dynamics that are in Etsy's control, we see a solid runway for international growth, but improvements in search quality remains a "show me" story. We like that Etsy is leaning into self-funded promotions and view this as another tool in the tool kit that could reduce Etsy's need to compete head-to-head with Temu in ad channels. That said, we fall firmly into the camp that in order for Etsy to durably reaccelerate GMS growth it must reaccelerate the top of the funnel. To do this, barring a better than expected impact from search improvements, Etsy will require a step-up in marketing spend which in turn threatens EBITDA margin expansion in '24 (St modeling 40bps of leverage) from cost savings efforts associated with the RIF at core Etsy and broader efficiency opportunities at the subsidiary level.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Staples

Mark Carden on Sysco Corp: We have a Buy rating on Sysco. We think the company is set up to see improving market share gains in the year ahead. Its local case growth should improve as it adds headcount to its sales force and removes its compensation caps. The distributor should also benefit from further maturation in its Sysco Your Way initiative (more frequent service in restaurant dense neighborhoods), its growing loyalty program, and its ability to cross sell (e.g. its Edward Don acquisition gives it an opportunity to cross sell foodservice and equipment). Plus, macro concerns (especially deflation) are starting to ease. Still, it trades a standard deviation below its 5-year average NTM EV/EBITDA, which we believe is too steep of a discount.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Energy

Josh Silverstein on EOG Resources Inc: We have a Buy rating on EOG. Best in class balance sheet with \$1Bn of net cash that will be used to drive higher shareholder returns in the current crude oil price environment, support shareholder returns through a downcycle, or be used to improve acquisition economics. Strong operator with deep inventory across the core Lower48 basins.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Financials

Brian Meredith on Assurant Inc: We have a Buy rating on Assurant. AIZ is benefiting from quicker than expected profitability improvement in its Global Housing segment, and we expect revenue growth and profitability to improve in its Global Lifestyles segment in 2024. As growth and margins improve, we expect the shares to re-rate higher back to historical averages.

Brian Meredith on MetLife Inc: We have a Buy rating on MetLife. We believe MET’s Group growth and profitability trends should remain favorable while Int'l growth / earnings could come in above expectations. We also view the company's capital position favorably despite its above peer CRE exposure (which we view as manageable). As fears over its CRE exposure dissipate, we see its shares re-rating back to historical levels.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Financials

Alex Kramm on FactSet Research Systems Inc: We are Neutral on FactSet. While multiyear investments seem to have driven the company to a higher sustainable growth rate, a tougher end market environment has increased uncertainty around near- and medium-term targets again. Management recently lowered its FY24 guidance, but it is too early to know if the outlook has been fully de-risked.

Timothy Chiodo on PayPal Holdings Inc: Our fundamental view is that we are Neutral on the stock given gross profit growth challenges associated with both the core Branded Checkout button and the impacts associated with the migration of three legacy business (PayPal Pro, PayPal Here, and PayPal Invoices). While we expect continued gross profit growth pressures (albeit partially due to factors that will either partially lap or become less meaningful into 2024), uncertainty around Opex levels (previously guided to decrease in 2024 under prior management), and increasing competitive pressures (longer-term) to limit the attractiveness of the shares near-term, we could see a path for PayPal to become more attractive if we are able to gain confidence in a resumption of more meaningful total company gross profit growth.

Brennan Hawken on Raymond James Financials Inc: We are Neutral on the stock. RJF continues to face headwinds from increasing deposit costs, and its HY offering is still less penetrated at 25% of deposits (vs roughly 40% for MS and 50% for SF), suggesting those headwinds could sustain for at least the NT. Separately organic growth (i.e. NNA) has slowed recently, with no clear path to reacceleration.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Health Care

Kevin Caliendo on Cardinal Health Inc: We have a Buy rating on Cardinal. CAH continues to see solid momentum within Pharma Distribution, buoyed by stable Rx market trends, ramp of nascent specialty offerings, growth of largest customers and upside from vaccine distribution. The Medical Segment remains a longer-term turnaround story; we believe shares can re-rate on the back of above-market Pharma growth and potential for upside if the company can hit its targets in Medical.

AJ Rice on Centene Corp and Molina Healthcare: The biggest near-term uncertainty for Centene and Molina relates to the on-going process of Medicaid reverifications, which has been playing out since last summer. Medicaid represents 50% of revenue for Centene and 80% for Molina. We should finally get clarity on this issue in the March to June time frame. We are Neutral because of this and, in the case of Centene, concern that the expectations for 2025 may be too high as well (but that will take time to play out).

Kevin Caliendo on McKesson Corp: MCK’s diverse business model continues to be a differentiator, providing the ability to capture improved Rx economics from multiple angles. GLP-1 momentum has been boosting both US Pharma and Prescription Services, adding a dual-lever growth driver to near-term forecasts. Further, a strong balance sheet continues to be a cushion.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Health Care

Trung Huynh on Pfizer Inc: We have a Neutral rating on Pfizer. We expect sentiment on Pfizer remains low. While the company has guided for 2024, there remain question marks on the base business, COVID, and margins. Additionally we do not see many meaningful catalysts in 2024 that are able to change the narrative on the company. We would need to see successful execution across the newly launched products to be more comfortable on company fundamentals for 2024. However, we are positive on the Seagen deal (Padcev 1L bladder), and the dividend yield (6%), especially with a decline of rates over the year. Most debate stems from what to pay for the stock with bears at 8-10x vs. bulls 10-12x (UBS 10x 2025 PE).

Kevin Caliendo on Henry Schein Inc: We have a Neutral rating on Henry Schein. The company is going through the impact of a cyber attack. There is more uncertainty now due to potential of lost share.

Trung Huynh on Bristol-Myers Squibb Co: We have a Neutral rating on the stock. We expect interest in Bristol-Myers Squibb to remain low following a disappointing year (YTD -28.7% vs. peers -5.1% & S&P500 +24.2%). As we enter 2024, we think it'll be tough to change the narrative anytime soon despite a new CEO or management's initiatives to enhance R&D innovation with a string of recently announced deals. The stock is trading at 7x 2025 PE, while unlikely this multiple is expected to trade lower, we struggle to see multiple expansion through catalysts or execution from growth drivers, which have been slower to take off than expected. While Camzyos has seen an uptick in adoption, we see near-term competition on the horizon following recent positive results from aficamten. We believe BMY needs to continue to pursue BD to boost growth (recent acquisitions of Mirati, Karuna, and Rayze are prime examples). However, at a PE of 7x our 2025e EPS, we believe the company is appropriately valued to reflect lower growth relative to peers.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Industrials

Kevin McVeigh on Ceridian HCM Holding: We have a Buy rating on the stock. We find CDAY's valuation more attractive given its greater enterprise exposure, product leadership, and tech stack - factors which should drive top-line resilience amid margin expansion on mix, implementation changes, and back- office efficiencies. CDAY is a UBS top pick for 2024.

Thomas Wadewitz on CSX Corp: We have a Buy rating on CSX. We expect CSX's strong rail network operating performance to enable share gain vs truck and we also expect its favorable leverage to chemicals to support volume growth. Valuation is also attractive for CSX which is trading around its 5-year average P/E of 17.7x in contrast to most other transports which are at premium P/Es.

Steven Fisher on Paccar Inc: We are Neutral on the stock. We expect a truck market downturn in 2024, which will be balanced by PCAR’s relative stability (strong balance sheet, low decrementals) and continued expansion in the parts business. Good execution, and visibility to solid production for the next few quarters should support earnings and limit downside. The bear case is that orders fall significantly over the next few months, driving downside for 2024, and that TPO margins fall back notably below 18%. The bull case is that freight activity reaccelerates, and freight rates start to improve in 2024, and PCAR earnings could see a strong upcycle starting in late 2024.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Industrials

Gavin Parsons on RTX Corp: We are Neutral on RTX. We see risk skewed to the downside, with pressure from pension/interest expense/taxes/no buybacks, and 2025 guidance/consensus still look too high. We expect an update on GTF, but believe it is still early for a significant de-risking. The valuation discount has narrowed.

Gavin Parsons on Howmet Aerospace Inc: We are Neutral on HWM. We see continued momentum in commercial OE with the potential for upside to build rates (particularly MAX). Forged Wheel headwinds look reflected in consensus.

Kevin McVeigh on Paychex Inc: Despite recent weakness, we remain Neutral as we don't think the stock is discounting enough macro concern even as Paychex reaffirmed FY-24 6-7% revenue growth. As such, we continue to call for rotation out of temporary help [ASGN + RHI] as cyclical concerns drive relative HCM outperformance on more durable estimates + multiples on easing 10y treasury yields.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Info Tech

Kevin McVeigh on Accenture plc: Accenture is poised to expand its moat as the leader in digital IT Services through innovation, successful recruitment of headcount, technology partnerships and tuck-in acquisitions. That said, current valuation drives our Neutral rating.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Info Tech

Timothy Arcuri on Seagate Technology Holdings: We have a Neutral rating on the stock. Demand remains sluggish, though STX is seeing some gradual improvement in nearline demand from cloud customers, and HAMR is an idiosyncratic story for STX (should be accretive to both share and margins). The bigger concern is still the pace of the recovery in overall HDD demand where we see things remaining slow off the bottom as the shift in spending to "mission critical" AI related infrastructure seems unlikely to swing back to traditional white box infrastructure very quickly.

David Vogt on IBM: We are Sell rated on IBM with a \$120 PT or 12x our CY25 EPS of \$9.97 vs Consensus \$10.41 because 1) Slowing growth in key verticals like Red Hat and Consulting in 2024 should impact consolidated revenue growth. As such we forecast just \~2% revenue and EPS growth while the shares trade at 16x NTM earnings, and 2) Pre-tax income margins in 2024 are unlikely to expand consistent with 2023 as roughly two-thirds of the expansion last year was driven by a one-time accounting change that does not repeat.

Kevin McVeigh on Cognizant Tech Solutions: We are Neutral on the stock. With favorable positioning following significant investments in digital IT Services, Cognizant should at least shadow its share of industry-wide IT Services spending, which is expected to grow in the high single digits over the next five years.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Materials

Joshua Spector on PPG Industries Inc: We have a Buy rating on PPG. We see further price/cost benefits to drive higher margins over the next year. We also see volumes as near a trough level, which provides downside protection and higher potential for recovery in a stronger macro environment.

Joshua Spector on Linde Plc: We have a Buy rating on Linde. We expect LIN to continue to deliver stable volumes, with projects offsetting a softer macro. We expect earnings can grow due to further cost savings and residual positive price/costs.

Joshua Spector on Avery Dennison Corp: We have a Buy rating on the stock. AVY’s volumes have already been sequentially increasing through 2H23, and we expect this to continue into 2024. We also see an inflection in RFID sales as a positive support lever for growth into 2024.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Materials

Joshua Spector on FMC Corp: We are Neutral rated as we see continued weak crop chemical demand over 4Q/1Q, and ongoing uncertainty around crop chem pricing.

Joshua Spector on Albemarle Corp: We are Neutral rated, but skewed negative nearterm. We expect continued uncertainty in lithium prices against weaker EV demand. We also model earnings for 1Q24 and 2024 meaningfully below consensus, which is a negative risk.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Real Estate

Michael Goldsmith on Federal Realty Invs Trust: We have a Neutral rating on Federal Realty. FRT’s portfolio is concentrated in markets with strong demographic profiles. We expect better customer demand for retail located in areas with stronger demographics given increased pressure on the consumer. This should contribute to elevated tenant demand, occupancy, and lease spreads.

Michael Goldsmith on AvalonBay Communities Inc: We have a Neutral rating on AvalonBay. The company’s Coastal portfolio should continue to deliver same store growth in the top end of the peer group given favorable supply/demand dynamics. Normalizing bad debt should be a tailwind for AVB in 2024. Additionally, we expect strong value creation from the development pipeline.

Robin Farley on Host Hotels & Resorts: We are Neutral on HST with an \$18 price target. HST is the only hotel REIT with an investment grade balance sheet which positions it well to be a net acquirer of assets this year, along with reinvesting in its properties and continuing to buy back shares and pay a dividend. HST has done an admirable job postpandemic of delivering margin growth versus 2019, but we see risk to margins as HST has seen rates come down from peak levels.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Real Estate

Michael Goldsmith on Mid-America Apartment: We have a Sell rating on MAA. We expect demand to hold up for Sunbelt Apartment properties, but we see supply pressure limiting pricing power through 2025. Deliveries in 2024 for the US should be 500k units followed by 460k in 2025 with a higher concentrating in the Sunbelt. The pre-COVID average was 300k units per year.

Michael Goldsmith on UDR Inc: We have a Neutral rating on UDR. While we expect UDR’s Coastal properties to perform relatively well, we expect significant pressure on its Sunbelt properties which comprise 25% of SSNOI. Additionally, unlike other Coastal Apartment REITs, UDR does not have a tailwind in 2024 from bad debt.

Michael Goldsmith on Public Storage: We have a Neutral on PSA. Demand for Self Storage space continues to be choppy. Additionally, competitive pricing strategies are impacting rental rates. Storage was a top performing REIT subsector in 4Q’23, bringing valuations relative to the REIT industry for the group up to the historical average.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Utilities

Ross Fowler on NRG Energy Inc: We are Neutral on NRG. Supported by sale of STP nuclear plant for \$1.2 billion and accelerated share repurchase of \$950 million with settlement in 1Q’24. In process of proving Smart Home business model and execution of cross-selling and \$250 million of cost cutting goals by 2025.

Ross Fowler on CMS Energy Corp: Consistently delivers top quartile EPS growth of 7.5% in constructive Michigan regulatory environment. Business mix is substantially regulated (97% ’22 EPS) and does not require equity in 2024. Catalyst is electric rate decision in 1Q’24. We have a Buy rating on CMS.

Gregg Orrill on Edison International: We have a Buy rating on EIX. Path established to 6% EPS growth to 2028 supported by California load growth in electrification and EVs. California regulation offers predictable outcomes and is above average. Trades at 13x ’25 UBS EPS estimate of \$5.72 which reflects 4 years of maximum fire outlays due to state insurance fund caps.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Utilities

Ross Fowler on Pinnacle West Capital: We have a Sell rating on PNW. Arizona has long been one of the most difficult regulatory jurisdictions, largely due to the public utilities commission being elected officials. PNW is awaiting the outcome of a critical case, but may need to file another case rather quickly. The PNW service territory has aboveaverage growth, but the company will need to manage costs and regulatory outcomes.

Ross Fowler on Dominion Energy Inc: We have a Neutral rating on Dominion. The company is attempting to sell down a large stake of its regulated offshore wind project, which has been a challenging prospect sector wide. The company has had two major strategic shifts in the last three plus years and investors need clarity that management can deliver on the new plan, once it is finalized.

Ross Fowler on Eversource Energy: We have a Neutral rating on Eversource. The longpending offshore wind sale announcement presents material concerns around the amount of proceeds ES can generate and the corresponding impact that will have on the amount of equity the company potentially may need to issue, and thus the longer-term EPS outlook. The Connecticut regulatory jurisdiction also presents challenges for ES, as it ranks as one of the least constructive jurisdictions in the UBS regulatory rankings.

## What our European (fundamental) sector analysts think about our top/ bottom picks?

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Communication Services

Ondrej Cabejsek on Telia Co AB: We recently upgraded Telia to Buy with a SEK32.0 PT. Telia is among the most exposed to a normalising environment, particularly interest rates and energy costs, that is now starting to amplify its consistent top line execution (2-4% telecom LTM service revenue growth). We believe Telia can deliver on its MSD organic EBITDA growth guidance, that it can deleverage by c0.5x by YE24 (from 2.53x ND/ EBITDA at 3Q23), and that it will start to cover its dividends again in 2024 after which DPS will compound at the original c5% p.a. target. We see no major strategic changes from new management and expect it to deliver TV transformation (content/ restructuring/M&A), a key catalyst.

Adam Berlin on Publicis Groupe: We have a Buy rating on Publicis. We see a pivot to non-comms services providing structurally higher growth rates and less cyclicality in the medium term and vs. its peers. Publicis trades at an attractive multiple (11x PE), with net cash.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Communication Services

Polo Tang on Koninklijke KPN NV: We are Neutral on KPN. We see the company as well managed and think the story is attractive longer-term when fibre rollout is complete and capex normalises (2027 onwards). However, in the near term, the AMX exchangeable acts as a ‘magnet’ for KPN shares give delta hedging into maturity (link). The exchangeable covers 17% of KPN shares and matures on 2 March 2024 with a €3.12 strike price. Separately, rising fibre wholesale costs/tax/interest is weighing on 2024 estimates and we are wary that the environment could get more promotional in the near term – see our field trip feedback note (pages 10-13) detailing the key issues in the Dutch market (link).

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Consumer Discretionary

Chris Huang on Brunello Cucinelli Spa: We have a Buy rating on Brunello. We believe 2024 will once again showcase BC's superior fundamentals, highlighted by the overall challenging macro and slowing sector context. We expect the company to deliver +11% organic sales growth in 2024 and continue its +MSD% outperformance vs. the sector, thanks to the brand's absolute luxury positioning which entails strong pricing power and resilient volume growth. This combined with more meaningful operating leverage from 2024 onwards as BC exceeds the €1bn sales mark, drive our EPS estimates \~MSD% above cons with +low-teens% 2023-26 CAGR. (For more, see Brunello Cucinelli S.p.A "2024 Outlook: Weaving exquisite yet resilient growth")

Jarrod Castle on Whitbread Plc: We continue to be constructive on Whitbread as we believe the company can continue to grow profits in the next financial year, Germany represents an opportunity for further growth and we see the case for M&A and/or shareholder return. We have a Buy rating on the stock.

David Lesne on Michelin: We have a Buy rating on the stock. Michelin offers a combination of (1) upside risk to 2024 consensus with EBIT approaching €4bn (c10% above consensus), (2) good visibility on FCF despite the macro uncertainty (>10% yield), and (3) a low valuation vs its historical average (c8.5x PE vs 10x average).

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Consumer Discretionary

Yashraj Rajani on Zalando SE: We think Zalando remains the best-in-class Online pure play fashion player in Europe, but we think expectations for 2024 and 2025 both on GMV growth and adj. EBIT margins remain full. Recent Textilwirtschaft data and Germany Retail sales suggest some weakness in Nov/Dec sales. This along with soft consumer sentiment in Germany keeps us cautious. With limited catalysts for returning Online penetration and ZAL’s topline growth, we remain Neutral.

Zuzanna Pusz on Burberry Group: We have a Sell rating on Burberry. For many years BRBY has been the sector's most debated turnaround, which in 2023 entered a new chapter with high hopes for Daniel Lee's first collection. However, the increasingly challenging sector context, combined with so far muted reaction of the trade and consumer to the new brand aesthetics, means that the turnaround may have to be more costly to succeed. Since the appointment of Lee as the creative director in Sep 2022, the market has had high hopes for the brand's new direction expected to drive HSD OSG in the MT. However, the feedback on the new collection seems muted. Our discussions with selected wholesalers suggest its price point is too high for the targeted consumer, thus driving a reduction in orders y/y, while some of the social media trends also don't suggest any "hype" among the consumers. As such with sector growth slowing, BRBY may be in a tough spot with its higher exposure to the cyclical RTW category (\~60% of sales) and wholesale (18% of sales). BRBY's previous attempted turnaround under the direction of CEO Gobbetti centred on costs, with the company having successfully cut \~15% of its addressable cost base. This together with a +VE FX impact allowed it to achieve a 20.5% EBIT margin as of FY23 (vs. 16.6% in FY17). However, years of cost control in an increasingly competitive and polarised luxury sector, means that the company may need to step up its spending and gross margin reinvestments, to drive a LT sustainable profitability improvement. Otherwise we believe it risks being stuck in its \~ £500m EBIT 10-year range.

Saranja Sivachelvam on Kingfisher Plc: It is our top Sell rating, because 1) Sales: top three markets are in structural decline, 2) Profit: consensus and many bulls on the buy side expect KGF to return to peak margins, which it hasn’t been able to do despite all the self help in the past two years, and 3) FCF: company guidance for a FCF of £500-600m is too punchy in the future, in our view, given that KGF has never accomplished this before and there isn’t necessarily a catalyst beyond this year (inventory reversal) to suggest that it can do so.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Staples

Jo Barnet-Lamb on HelloFresh SE: We have a Sell rating on the stock. The nature of HFG's model gives it weaker cohort dynamics vs eFood peers. These issues were masked through COVID where the group benefited from low CAC even as it deployed larger marketing budgets. Now the group is seeing both a resumption of cohort churn and rising CACs. We built a detailed cohort model exposing the demanding expectations in consensus, specifically that the group can deploy +4%/+7% YoY absolute marketing in '24/'25 whilst keeping CAC inflation below trend. We believe the group will have a marketing (and therefore profit) vs customer acquisition (and therefore revenue) trade off; with either outcome likely to be perceived as an existential threat to sustainable profitability. Until consensus calibrates marketing vs customer additions correctly, we see risk to consensus estimates and therefore HFG shares. Whilst valuation may seem undemanding, HFG’s main profit center, US meal-kit, is still declining double-digits (with no sign of meaningful improvement), making the shares still expensive in our view.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Energy

Anna Kishmariya on Orlen SA: Orlen is the largest energy company in Poland. While the extensive investment plan over the next \~5 years provides for a negative FCF yield on our assumptions, the group has low leverage (ND/EBITDA at 0.2x in 3Q23) and reiterated its commitment to the base dividend which should yield 6.6% for FY23 and 6.8% for FY24. The recent change in Polish government is broadly well-received by the market, especially for SOEs, but for a meaningful re-rating of Orlen shares in our view we would need to see an update on the group’s strategy or projects planned. We are Neutral on the stock.

Henri Patricot on Repsol SA: We rate Repsol Buy as we see the company's high-grading and rationalisation of assets as strategically sound. It is also diversifying its exposure in a range of low-carbon initiatives that are complementary to other parts of its business. We think the returns it is offering to shareholders are attractive at a 2023-24E distribution yield of \~14% vs the sector average of 12%. At our more conservative cashflow forecast (vs the company), its 2023/24E FCF yield of \~19% is still competitive vs. peers at 13/14%. We see near-term momentum as production in Upstream finally rebounds and refining margins remain high in the near term.

Henri Patricot on ENI Spa: We have a Buy rating on ENI. Eni has meaningfully transformed over the past 6 years, de-risking the investment proposition in terms of upstream portfolio mix (geographic and political), the balance sheet and the business mix. Eni has since invested in its low carbon activities and made progress in developing its renewable projects towards targets set. It has also simplified its shareholder returns structure which we think is helpful in providing investors more clarity on how it plans to return cash to the market. We think the shares are still the cheapest of the middle tier of European Integrateds.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Energy

Henri Patricot on Equinor Asa: We are Neutral on the stock. Equinor is effectively an E&P company, rather than a true Integrated but has a portfolio of assets by virtue of scale, quality and diversity that resembles a Major. The wind business it has built over >10 years is one of the few examples of 'big oil' having established a material position in the renewables space and we incorporate that into the valuation. In shareholder returns, it commits US\$17bn on capital distribution for 2023. Equinor's distribution yield is competitive at 18/12% vs peers of \~12% for 2023/24E but not supported by FCF generation (FCF yield only \~11%/8%).

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Financials

Nasib Ahmed on Aviva Plc: Buy rated and most preferred life insurance focused name in the UK given high capital return (inc. buybacks) and diversification. Aviva is the only composite insurer in the UK which allows it to grow in markets where the cycle results in the greatest ROC with capital diversification on top.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Financials

Justin Forsythe on Worldline SA: We have a Sell rating on the stock. We think numbers for 2024 are not fully derisked, and on a cash flow basis the valuation appears more reasonable. Also a full fledged take out is upside risk, however looks likely it’s a French solution / minority stake / backing by existing partners, less likely to result in M&A related upside compared to Nexi. Upside case is it just hits numbers and operational risks don’t materialise with 200m restructuring plans.

Haley Tam on Hargreaves Lansdown Plc: We have a Sell rating on HL. We see downside risk to consensus earnings from: i) Regulation. The FCA is highly focused on interest income retention by platforms on client cash, and has set end Feb 2024 deadlines for change; ii) Competition. HL charges higher fees than the majority of its competitors, and this continues to be a headwind for new customer acquisition and net fund flows.

Jason Napier on HSBC: We are Neutral on the stock. HSBC has performed well in recent years buoyed by the benefits of higher policy rates in the US in particular which feed through to the bank’s Hong Kong operations. With \~\$800bn in excess deposits and tangible equity, HSBC is inevitably positively geared to higher interest rates and vulnerable to falling revenues when rates normalise. Our net interest income forecasts for 2025 are 7% below consensus and are based on just 150bps in rate cuts by end 2025. We do expect HSBC to return large amounts of capital via dividends and buybacks (helped by the disposal of its Canadian operations). We expect the share price to be capped by persistent downgrades to forecast aggregate earnings. We don’t think it will pay to fight the Fed, unless it turns out that the Fed will not cut rates as implied by current yield curves.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Health Care

Graham Doyle on Convatec Group: We have a Buy rating on the stock. Convatec is a turnaround story that we believe is becoming a quality growth story set to deliver a >6% mid-term organic revenue CAGR and mid teens EPS growth.

Xian Deng on Ipsen: We have a Buy rating on Ipsen. It is a midcap pharma company that grows at above sector rate but trades at a discount, largely driven by approved assets. We like Dysport (Ipsen’s botox equivalent for cosmetics use), and we believe its consumer characteristics could drive sustained long-term growth. The longevity is often underappreciated and it is what makes Dysport the biggest asset by NPV in our model.

Graham Doyle on Fresenius Se & Co: We have a Buy rating on the stock. FRE is a turnaround story where management has shown it is executing (EPS upgrades, balance sheet deleveraging) and we expect the multiple to begin reflecting this.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Health Care

Colin White on Sanofi: We are Neutral on Sanofi. By the end of its exclusivity, Dupixent will account for over 1/3 of Sanofi's revenues and thus the question of replacement ability will probably remain a meaningful variable in the way the market values the stock. Achieving Sanofi's ambition of launching 3-5 new products with €2-5bn peak sales potential each between 2026 and 2030 would clearly provide the replacement power it needs; the market though remains sceptical of this ambition. Sanofi’s decision to lower mid-term targets and increase R&D spending to back development of some late-stage assets was not received favourably. The challenge for Sanofi now is to convince investors that they made the right choice in backing these assets.

Colin White on Roche Holdings: We have a Neutral rating on Roche. Underlying growth in 2023 is decent which will be reflected in the growth momentum in 2024 once the base effect is tucked in. However, due to a number of pending biosimilar headwinds, this growth is not easily sustainable and we expect EPS to hover in the low CHF20 range for some time. That's because there is little in the medium-term pipeline we can point to that would make a meaningful difference.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Industrials

Sven Weier on Signify NV: We are Neutral on the stock. Following last year’s share price underperformance the shares could be in for some mean reversion as the market might think the worst is behind it. However, we see a potential downturn in non-residential construction that has not played out yet as still being the biggest risk for the company.

Rory McKenzie on SPIE SA: We have a Buy rating on SPIE, a defensive compounder with exposure to sustainability-driven themes, trading at an attractive valuation. Recent results showed organic growth is still tracking ahead of expectations, and we see a c5% organic CAGR achievable over the mid-term given the majority of revenues are generated from spending on e.g. improving building efficiency, the energy transition, and connectivity. With scope for continued bolt-on M&A in fragmented markets, we therefore see a potential 8-9% adj net income CAGR that looks undervalued today on c11x EV/EBITA.

Gregor Kuglitsch on Vinci SA: We have a Buy rating on the stock. Vinci offers exposure to inflation-linked concessions and the energy transition on the contracting side. The shares offer a c9% equity IRR at the current share price. We view the large and highly diversified portfolio of Vinci as a defensive play.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Industrials

Nicole Manion on Rentokil Initial Plc: We are Sell-rated on Rentokil as we take a more cautious view on the pace of margin progress in the key North America region. After the company pushed its NA margin target for FY’23 at Q3 results due to underlying market pressures, we see risks that investment needs in Terminix exceed what targets/consensus allows for – due to the scale and complexity of the route/branch optimisation, the need to mend significant historical underperformance within TMX, and a generally challenging market backdrop in which to execute on such a sizeable integration. We sit -6% below consensus profits next year, and after a disappointing Q3 we expect year-end to continue to highlight i) the organic gap vs peer Rollins, and ii) the challenge of the integration ahead.

Marcus Cole on Travis Perkins Plc: We think prospects for Travis Perkins look challenging due to: (1) limited pricing power, (2) Toolstation Europe continues to be a drag on profits, (3) private peers appear to be aggressive on taking share, and (4) leverage limits optionality. We are Neutral on the stock.

Sebastian Vogel on Belimo Holdings AG: We have a Sell rating on the stock. We expect that Belimo’s focus on the European non-residential construction sector will be a burden for the group’s top-line growth in the near term. Moreover, our expected weakened sales growth might serve as a litmus test for the company’s structural demand function, which should serve as an additional burden for the company’s medium-term outlook. In the meantime, the stock trades on a P/E (next year) of around 38x, hence discounting not much of these potential negatives, in our view.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Info Tech

Francois-Xavier Bouvignies on Infineon Technologies: We have a Buy rating on Infineon. Content semis growth is driving the earning momentum in the next few years fo Infineon due to EV penetration increasing.

Michael Briest on Capgemini SE: We have a Buy rating on the stock. In the highly fragmented market for IT Services, Capgemini is a global, scale player. It can adapt its skills base rapidly given its high offshore footprint, which also supports a competitive margin profile.

Kathinka de Kuyper on Bechtle AG: We are Neutral on the stock. Bechtle is the largest IT system house in Germany and is increasing its presence in Europe through acquisitions. We expect Q4 to be the inflection point in revenue growth on the back of strong order intake in Q3 driven by public sector clients and large accounts. Cash flow is strong on the back of an improvement in inventory levels.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Info Tech

Harry Blaiklock on Nordic Semiconductor ASA: On Nordic, in terms of fundamental view: we see risk of sales growth taking longer to bounce back in 2024 and believe the market is currently overestimating Nordic’s long-term growth potential. Pricing has remained stable so far but any significant swing back after the large increases we saw in 2021/22 could drive further downside beyond our base case. We have a Sell rating on the stock.

Andre Kukhnin on Hexagon AB: While we favour the company’s organic growth profile, software-hardware business mix and scope for margin expansion from an already very healthy level, we see these as largely reflected in the current valuation, hence leaving potential for only modest further upside. We are Neutral on the stock.

Rory Smith on Spectris Plc: Destocking in Life Sciences heeds caution on Spectris, to which we add signs of Auto capex weakness. We are Neutral on the stock.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Materials

Gregor Kuglitsch on Heidelberg Materials AG: We are Neutral on the stock. Heidelberg Materials looks lowly valued on around 7x P/E and >10% FCF yield. Pricing dynamics in cement look reasonably positive and with the backdrop of declining energy input costs, we think earnings should remain resilient at high levels. The key risk is capital allocation, with management signalling it would consider a larger M&A transaction, although for the time being nothing has materialised.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Materials

Priyanka Patel on K+S AG: We are Sell rated on K+S given it is a high cost producer of potash (70% of production in Germany) and short term we see no recovery in the potash price and thus earnings with unsupportive farmer economics as well as limited FCF generation. K+S also faces longer-term earnings risk due to increasing supply in the potash industry from BHP (2026) and Anglo American (2027).

Daniel Major on Boliden AB: We are Neutral on BOl. The company has faced a series of operational issues over the past 12-18 months that have led to reduced near-term production/earnings and material increases in capex. We have low conviction that BOL has overcome all of these operational challenges and high capex will result in limited/-ve FCF and a material increase in net debt will limit cash returns for the foreseeable future.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Real Estate

Charles Boissier on Unibail Rodamco Wesfield: We are Neutral on Unibail. Prime dominant malls’ key metrics (footfall, retail sales, etc) are now above pre-pandemic levels in Europe. Our recent field trip of the company’s London and Paris malls - its two largest submarkets in Europe - showed strong operations and management is pointing to resilient capital values now.

Charles Boissier on Warehouse De Pauw: We have a Buy rating on WDP. European logistics continues to enjoy a number of structural tailwinds (i.e. near-shoring, ecommerce, etc), from which WDP is benefiting through robust operations and stabilising valuations.

Charles Boissier on Merlin Properties: We have a Buy rating on Merlin. Occupancy has just reached an all-time high, as Spain’s largest real estate company continues to enjoy strong indexation and better-than-European average economic growth, with future portfolio expansion likely coming from logistics and data center, where fundamentals are strong and management is not ruling out a potential strategic partnership.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Utilities

Wanda Serwinowska on Engie: We have a Buy rating on Engie. In our view, the reasons for Engie's historically low multiples are being addressed systematically, with: (i) volatile commodity-sensitive activities disposed; (ii) opaque energy services activities disposed; (iii) a nuclear exit pathway agreed in Belgium, with liabilities capped at last; (iv) a company-wide cost & simplification programme under way; (v) new multi-year earnings and dividend guidance issued; and (vi) a management focus on consistent delivery. The result should be a more stable and predictable business that no longer deserves the current discount to utility sector multiples, but track records take time to be established and this is likely to be a multi-year process.

Gonzalo Sanchez-Bordona on Enel Spa: We have a Buy rating on Enel. We liked the plan presented in the recent CMD, with focus on cash generation, cost reduction, capex cuts and potential dividend upside. We see the company delivering higher than expected earnings growth through 2026 and with >8bn lower net capex than in the previous plan. Stock continues trading at an unjustified discount in light of superior dividend, attractive growth and balance sheet derisking.

Wanda Serwinowska on E.ON: We have a Buy rating on E.ON. Given macro uncertainty into 2024, we believe e.on represents a good value proposition given: (i) lower risk (75% EBITDA is regulated), (ii) strong balance sheet relative to peers, and (iii) the highest 2023- 27E DPS growth across regulated peers. The stock trades at a discount to its peers and any move to risk-off or rate-cutting scenarios in 2024 should add further upside, in our view.

## What our Chinese (fundamental) sector analysts think about our top/ bottom picks?

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Communication Services

Kenneth Fong on Tencent (TME UN): We maintain Neutral on TME mainly on its fair valuation. We expect the company to see solid growth in online music despite macro uncertainty, aided by strong momentum in subscription and recovery in ads, while this could be largely offset by weakness in social entertainment business. We are encouraged to see management's focus on cost controls, which could lead to improving gross and net margins in 2024E and potential margin upside in outer years. Post +38.4% rise in stock price in recent 3 months, the stock is trading at 16x/14x 2024E/25E PE, in line with its 2-year average.

Kenneth Fong on Kanzhun Ltd (BZ UW): We remain cautious on Boss near term, as we believe weak macroeconomic recovery would weigh on recruiting demand and limit growth upside in the near term. 4Q is a traditionally low season for job market as people wait until after Chinese New Year (CNY) to switch jobs, while the late CNY in 2024 could weigh on the seasonality of 1Q24 as well. We believe it takes time for investors to build conviction on the recovery trajectory for job market and Boss's growth in 2024. The stock is trading at 21.7x 2024E PE based on consensus estimates, near the high end in China Internet space but lower than its historical average.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Consumer Discretionary

Kenneth Fong on PDD Holdings (PDD UW): We have a Buy rating on PDD because it should see dual engines ahead: 1) Domestic marketplace business to remain resilient with continued market share gain, given PDD’s low price proposition (ASP <Rmb50 vs other platforms’ Rmb80-100), and 2) Temu offering upside potential with robust volume growth and improving UE. Valuation attractive at 19x 2024 P/E with 38% earnings growth over 2023-25.

Nora Min on Fuyao Glass Industry Group (3606 HK): We have a Buy rating on the stock. We think Fuyao is one of the few auto parts companies who have strong bargaining power, endowed by its over 30% global market share. The importance of bargaining power could become more prominent amid intensified industry competition. We expect Fuyao to deliver robust earnings growth in 2024E, as: 1) binding with all mainstream OEMs, Fuyao’s customer structure is well balanced; 2) with rising contribution of highvalue added product, such as all-glass roof, coating glass, and encapsulated glass, Fuyao’s ASP is likely to rise steadily.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in China Staples

Veronica Song on Yihai International Holding (1579 HK): We are Neutral on Yihai. With a deceleration in related-party sales (to Haidilao), we think Yihai's future growth depends on third-party sales (both 2C and 2B), which feature higher GPM but also face bigger challenges. We think key challenges include: 1) market demand shrank after Covid; 2) key products might enter a downcycle due to changing consumer preferences; and 3) pressure on ASP and margins as consumers become more price sensitive amid a slowing economic environment. We think near-term visibility remains low and believe it will take time for Yihai to improve the success rates of core SKUs and its pricing competitiveness in the convenience foods industry.

Christine Peng on Smoore International Holdings (6969 HK): We are Neutral on Smoore with PT HK\$6.87. Our 2024E/25E earnings forecasts are 26%/32% lower than Visible Alpha consensus as we remain cautious about Smoore's margin improvement potential amid faster growth of disposable products which have lower profitability than rechargeable products. Its R&D investment will likely maintain at a high level due to needs to invest in new products. We believe Smoore's revenue has arrived at an inflection point after reaching a bottom in 2023E. As potential flavor ban of mentholflavor e-cigarettes in US remains to be an overhang, fast growth of disposable products could be the main driver for its revenue. Our DCF-based (WACC: 10.0%) price target of HK\$6.87 implies 25x/22x 2024E/25E PER on a 5% 2023-25E EPS CAGR. We believe Smoore's valuation will be supported by its resumed revenue growth from 2024 in the highly fragmented still growing global next-generation tobacco. We remain positive towards its long-term growth outlook but feel the current valuation is fair.

Christine Peng on Nongfu Spring Co Ltd (9633 HK): We are Neutral with PT HK\$48.00. Rising revenue contribution from beverages, instead of water, could imply fashion risk given consumers’ fast changing preferences of beverage products, in our view. We maintain our Neutral rating on Nongfu and believe its strong execution capabilities have been fully reflected in its valuation premium against peers (39x 2024E PER versus peers 19x 2024 PER). We believe Nongfu’s strong branding and distribution capabilities, as well as rising health awareness of consumers, could underpin continuous growth of Oriental Leaf. The concern is about its >50% price premium against other tea drinks, limiting further channel penetration into lower-tier cities, in our view. We increased our earnings estimates by 7-8% during 2023E-25E. However, given RMB depreciation, we maintain our PT unchanged, implying 27x 2024E EV/EBITDA.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Financials

May Yan on Agricultural Bank of China (1288 HK): We recently upgraded ABC-H from Neutral to Buy. While we lower our forecast for ABC’s 2024E earnings growth from 5.2% to 2.7% due to major NIM headwinds that weigh on the overall industry, we still expect ABC to outperform its peers, supported by its strong loan growth owing to its competitive advantages in rural- and county-area banking, sufficient provision buffer, as well as strong incentives to replenish its core tier-1 capital. We slightly revised down the CoE from 16.6% to 15.5% to showcase ABC’s better earnings resilience in the economic downcycle. Under our three-stage DDM model, our price target for ABC-H was raised from HK\$3.10 to HK\$3.45. The implied P/BV multiple would be 0.50x (vs historical average at 0.58x).

Charles Zhou on PICC Property and Casualty (2328 HK): We recently increased our price target for PICC P&C to HK\$10.6 from HK\$10.0, after (1) revising up 2024/25E NPAT by 2%/2% to recalibrate growth target, and (2) lifting P/B to 0.84x from 0.82x on a more benign competitive landscape with tightened regulatory oversight (e.g. curb on aggressive price competition). Further, we see positive fund flow supported by stronger investors' preference (particularly insurance asset management firms) towards high dividend yield stocks. We upgraded our rating to Buy from Neutral given attractive total return of \~20% (capital appreciation + dividend).

## Lowest-scoring stocks in China Financials

Charles Zhou on New China Life Insurance (1336 HK): We have a Neutral rating on NCI and it is our less preferred name in the space. The main reasons are: 1) NCI has a relatively high value of new business (VNB) and embedded value (EV) sensitivity to investment return. The equity market and interest rate environment in China are still subject to uncertainty, 2) Agency productivity is low relative to listed peers, meaning that is growth is more headcount-driven. We highlight the massive recruitment model has become less effective, due to the diminishing demographic dividend and 3) Business quality is not ideal vs peers, as evidenced by its relatively low persistency ratio. NCI just tuned down its operating assumptions last year.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Health Care

Jun Deng on Hansoh Pharmaceutical Group (3692 HK): We have a Buy rating on Hansoh. We like the company as we expect it to strike more out-license deals to unlock the global commercial potential of its pipeline. Other catalysts include: 1) potential NDA approval for Ibrexafungerp in China; 2) sNDA filings for Aumolertinib and Inebilizumab in China; 3) ph3 trial initiation for GLP-1/GIP in China; 4) ph1 trials initiation for HS-20089 (B7-H4 ADC) and HS-20093 (B7-H3 ADC) overseas by GSK.

Chen Chen on Innovent Biologics (1801 HK): We like Innovent for its sales ramp-up of products and the progress of its key pipeline products. We are expecting its key candidate, mazdutide (GLP-1/GCGR), to submit NDA in 2024 for obesity and type 2 diabetes to NMPA. We are also expecting potential regulatory approvals by NMPA for fulzerasib (KRAS G12C) in 2L NSCLC 2024E and taletrctinib (IBI344, ROS1) NDA approval in 2L ROS1 fusion NSCLC, 2024/2025E.

Chen Chen on Akeso Inc (9926 HK): We like Akeso as we are expecting its further sales ramp-up of its key product, cadonilimab (PD-1/CTLA-4). Akeso also has a number of candidates to receive potential regulatory approvals in 2024, including ebdarokimab (IL-12/IL-23, AK101, in plaque psoriasis, H224/H125E); ivonescimab (PD-1/VEGF bsAb, AK112, in 2L nsqNSCLC H224E); and ebronucimab (PCSK9, AK102, NDA in hypercholesterolemia, H224E).

## Lowest-scoring stocks in China Health Care

Chen Chen on Hangzhou Tigermed Consulting (3347 HK): We have a Neutral rating to Tigermed as we think it has high China revenue exposure and the weaker biopharma funding environment in China impacted the demand from outsourcing service. There is currently yet a clear sign of biopharma funding recovery and we think Tigermed’s operation recovery visibility is still low.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Industrials

Wei Shen on Weichai Power Co Ltd (2338 HK): We have a Buy rating on Weichai as we expect the company to deliver 20% earnings growth in 2024-25 due to solid truck demand, market share gain, and new business contribution.

Perry Yeung on Boc Aviation Ltd (2588 HK): We have a Buy rating on BOCA. On top of favourable aircraft leasing demand environment on the back of strong airlines' earnings outlook, potential peaking of interest rate in 2024E could drive net lease yield and ROE recovery.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in China Industrials

Sara Wang on China Communications Services (552 HK): We have a Buy rating on CCS because of its resilient profit growth at mid-high single digit with dividend yield at 8% 2024 dividend yield.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Info Tech

Thompson Wu on Xiaomi Corp (1810 HK): We have a Buy rating on Xiaomi. Overall, we expect China’s smartphone replacement cycles to reaccelerate into 2024, and believe Xiaomi benefits from this demand cycle improvement. We believe Xiaomi will push into more premium Android segments and offline channel investments can help improve profits over the cycle as well. Lastly, we believe Xiaomi’s Smart EV is an important catalyst for share price and we are nearing launch in Q224E.

Sara Wang on ZTA Corp (763 HK): With stalling telco capex, peaking gross margin, and overhang from Huawei competition, we believe share price would remain under pressure in the near term. We have a Neutral rating on the stock.

Wendy Zhang on Kingdee International (268 HK): We have a Buy rating on the stock. Kingdee is the second largest Enterprise Resource Planning (ERP) software provider in China and we expect 2023-25 revenue to deliver 17% CAGR benefiting from foreign vendor substitution (eg, Oracle) and enterprise digital transformation. After cloud transition, we expect Kingdee’s operating cash flow to achieve 50% CAGR growing from Rmb600m in 2023E to Rmb1.5bn in 2025, with net profit breakeven by 2025.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in China Info Tech

Grace Chen on AAC Technologies Holdings (2018 HK): We have a Sell rating on AAC given its high exposure to smartphones, and expect Apple’s pricing pressure to continue to weigh on its margins. While the company expected a recovery in H223 with smartphone demand outlook stabilizing, we believe it will still take time before its diversification efforts (to AR/VR and auto LiDAR business) bear fruit.

Randy Abrams on Hua Hong Semiconductor (1347 HK): We recently changed our rating for Hua Hong up from Sell to Neutral. It had a very poor H223 so has hit the stock and their financials throughout H223. We aren’t overly bullish as the recovery will be slow & more competition in China from emerging foundries so only to Neutral but viewed it at 0.5x P/B near its trough and also should meet its low 4Q guidance and at least guide stabilization.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in China Materials

Xinlei Li on cement names: We remain cautious on the China cement sector due to unfavorable S/D dynamics. Without any signals of supply exits, we expect cement profitability to be under pressure in 2024E from fierce price competition. Among our coverage, we least favor South China cement names like CR Building Materials give they face the most supply pressures from cross-region capacity swap projects.

## What our Japanese (fundamental) sector analysts think about our top/ bottom picks?

## Highest-scoring stocks in Japan Communication Services

Kenji Yasui on Softbank Group: We have a Buy rating on Softbank as its values of AI related is not reflected in the share price.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Japan Consumer Discretionary

Kohei Takahashi on Suzuki Motor Corp: We have a Buy rating on Suzuki as the positive price and mix trend should continue in India and Japan.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Japan Consumer Discretionary

Takahiro Kazahaya on Fast Retailing Co Ltd: We are Neutral on Fast because 1) China business: Reopening benefit is the peak in 1Q of FY8/24. The market would have some concerns about China business 2Q and onward, 2) US/Europe business: given the size of business they will have growth opportunity in mid-term, and 3) Japan business: they are the largest clothing retailer in Japan. This business will remain a cash generator.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Japan Staples

Rei Ihara on Asahi Group Holdings: We have a Buy rating on Asahi because 1) Strong earnings in short term because of the topline in domestic beer business after alcohol tax change, and 2) market has already been cautious about conservative guidance fully, so guidance toward 2024 should not be surprising.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Japan Staples

Rei Ihara on Meiji Holdings Co Ltd: We are Neutral on Meiji. We expect that topline momentum of probiotic yogurt was getting worse from Oct to Dec. In 2024, new plants will start to work in China and fixed cost will increase. Still we cannot predict earnings clearly in 2024.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Japan Health Care

Kasumi Haruta on Chugai Pharmaceutical Co Ltd: We have a Buy rating on the stock. Chugai’s profit is likely to grow positive next year and onwards with Hemlibra growth and other new products. Its drug discovery platform/capability is re-valued at this moment. The company also has an asset of oral GLP-1 agonist orforglipron and it is in lead position in development.

Tomoko Yoshihara on Terumo Corp: We are Neutral on Terumo. 3Q OP is likely to beat the consensus due to the brisk demand for core products and the positive impact from upward revision of prices in Japan. The company may revise up FY3/24 guidance. The CEO changes from Apr 24 could be a positive catalyst due to more focus on acquisitions.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Japan Health Care

Kasumi Haruta on Eisai Co Ltd: We are Neutral on Eisai. Speed of ramp up for Alzheimer’s drug Leqembi is still slow and actual sales is likely to miss 3Q consensus. Company is scheduled to file SubQ formulation by 3/24 but the launch will be 3/25 at the earliest so we assume Leqembi is likely to continue slow ramp up for 1-2 years from now. Share price should be under pressure until we see the strong sales momentum.

Tomoko Yoshihara on M3 Inc: We are Neutral on M3. Online marketing services sales for pharmaceutical companies could be lower than the company guidance (double digit growth in Oct-Dec 23). Investors could feel that the risk to miss its full year guidance is getting higher at 3Q(Oct-Dec) FY3/24 results. We should note that outcome of TOB for Benefit One which may be released in mid-Jan before 3Q result.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Japan Info Tech

Kenji Yasui on Fujitsu Limited and Nec Corp: We have Buy ratings on both as domestic IT service likely continue to grow strongly.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Japan Info Tech

Shingo Hirata on Alps Alpine Co Ltd: We are Neutral on the stock. Although the company’s module/system business has been gradually recovering along with auto production recovery and price hikes, the company is losing market share in the smartphone camera component business for the US key customer.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Japan Real Estate

Kazufumi Takeuchi on Mitsui Fudosan: Top pick with a Buy rating. Developers, who are sellers of real estate, are likely to continue to have a relative advantage over J-REITs, which are buyers. Based on the theme of property sales fueling share buybacks, our top pick is developer Mitsui Fudosan. Our price target of ¥3,900 is based on estimated aftertax NAV of ¥6,460/share calculated using the market value of Mitsui Fudosan's property holdings and the present value of its development projects, to which we apply a theoretical discount of 39.6% derived using a residual earnings model and discounting unrealised gains on owned real estate in present value. The current share price is trading at about a 48% discount to NAV, which looks cheap.

## What our Australian (fundamental) sector analysts think about our top/ bottom picks?

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Consumer Discretionary

Shaun Cousins on JB HI-FI Ltd: Retain Neutral as while sales growth is expected to be stronger than expected, with recent trading updates (1Q24) better than expected due to a more resilient consumer than expected, we remain cautious about rising costs (labour, rentals, energy) which will weigh on EBIT margins. Valuation less compelling (FY24E P/E 15.0x) given earnings risk and modest longer-term growth outlook (limited store growth potential).

Tim Piper on GUD Holdings Ltd: We have a Buy rating on GUD. Near-term earnings leveraged to supply recovery in key OEMs (Ford, Toyota, etc) through APG business, Underlying Core Auto business largely non-discretionary and less exposed to consumer slowdown. Continued de-leveraging of the balance sheet over the next \~12 months will see further acquisition opportunities explored. Valuation undemanding at 13.9x P/E.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Australia Consumer Discretionary

Tim Piper on Bapcor Ltd: \~70%+ of group earnings generated from non-discretionary aftermarket auto trade channel, a rational industry with high barriers to entry. Both sellside and buy-side expectations associated with ‘Better than before’ transformation program conservative (<50% of targeted incremental FY25e EBIT benefit priced in), in our view this program represents the vast majority of potential medium-term earnings upside and valuation looks attractive if management executes on targeted savings.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Staples

Shaun Cousins on Endeavour Group Ltd: We have a Buy rating on the stock. The EDV share price has fallen due to rising regulatory risk in its gaming business, LFL sales growth below its peer in Retail, and rising debt levels & borrowing costs. We have reviewed the regulatory risk in detail and identified mitigating factors for EDV, with the current share price incorporating a significant decline in gaming EBIT from FY27E onwards. We also see further support as shifting capex strategy and capital recycling improves Hotels ROFE.

Apoorv Sehgal on Graincorp Ltd: Buy based on through-the-cycle earnings and scope for capital returns. We expect a likely working capital unwind over FY24E (as crop conditions normalise in dryer conditions) to support a net core cash position expanding from \$200m at 1H23 to \$460m by FY24E, or 30% of mcap, before likely \~\$104m of proceeds from sale of its 8.5% UMG stake. We expect GNC to return a portion of this cash to shareholders. Stock trading on \~5x through-the-cycle EBITDA looks relatively cheap.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Australia Staples

Shaun Cousins on Coles Group Ltd: Retain Neutral as while it is leveraged to the tailwinds of the rational Australian food retail market (volume to grow due to population growth, shift from eating out of home; dry grocery food inflation to remain elevated), it has (1) endured gross margin compression due to rising theft (more impacted than peers), (2) faces rising opex from online (Ocado) & supply chain (Witron) investments that are temporary yet increase in FY24E on FY23 while remaining elevated in FY25E & extend into FY26E, while (3) productivity initiatives are a positive yet not expected to fully offset rising labour costs. These headwinds weigh on the company as its 1yr fwd P/E multiple (20.5x) is elevated albeit below peer WOW (23.6x).

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Australia Financials

John Storey on National Australia Bank: We are Sell rated on the stock. Stock is up +19% in the past 6 months. BUT in a higher for longer rate environment despite an economic soft landing NIM could surprise on the downside given their relative funding mix. Second most expensive bank in the sector. 14x forward PE and 5.45% DY.

Scott Russell on ASX Ltd: We have a Sell rating on ASX because of 1) Consensus opex and capex expectations are likely too low as the company works to upgrade CHESS system over the next 5 years, 2) High EBIT margins likely to decline as competitive threat becomes clearer, and 3) Stock expensive at 24x vs global peers and vs 10Y average below 20x.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Health Care

Laura Sutcliffe on Telix Pharmaceuticals: We have a Buy rating on the stock because of its cashflow positive biotech on the cusp of launching a new asset later in 2024.

Laura Sutcliffe on CSL Ltd: We have a Buy rating on CSL based on its recovery from the economics of processing plasma products at pandemic-impacted prices.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Industrials

Nathan Reilly on Worley Ltd: We have a Buy rating on Worley. The company offers significant earnings leverage to the four-fold increase in global energy transition capex required to decarbonize by 2050. We believe the CP2 LNG contract could be worth A\$9- 11bn in revenue to Worley over the next 3-4 years, with the market significantly underappreciating this opportunity. We forecast a 3yr EPS CAGR of 24%, and have added this global energy transition and capex beneficiary to the UBS APAC Key Call List. CP2 LNG is an underappreciated game changer.

Nathan Reilly on Seven Group Holdings: We have a Buy rating on the stock. Strong endmarket activity across mining and infrastructure construction supports a positive outlook amongst the Industrial Services segment. The stock is offering double-digit earnings growth in FY24, with its strong operating leverage remaining attractive as the business continues to de-lever on better cash flow conversion. FY24 guidance upgraded on strong Industrial Services momentum

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Australia Industrials

Nathan Reilly on Monadelphous Group Ltd: We are Neutral on the stock. In our view, FY24 earnings recovery is priced in. We believe elevated commodity prices and increasing resource sector capex commitments should continue to support Monadelphous’s order intake but highlight that skilled labour shortages and ongoing inflationary pressures are still a challenge for the company. Monadelphous Group "AGM - Sales growth guidance in line with consensus expectations".

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Australia Info Tech

Apoorv Sehgal on Technology One: We are Neutral on the stock. We view TNE as a high quality stock, but the company’s medium-term financial targets (ARR and PBT margins) are priced in and the stock is trading on a rich multiple in our view.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Materials

Lee Power on James Hardie Industries: We have a Buy rating on JHX because 1) Lead manufacturer of fibre cement in the US which is a growth and market penetration story in the US, 2) Exposure to strong US housing structural themes with significant undersupply of housing providing a higher earnings floor for a cyclical, 3) Trades in line with US new home builders but >50% of North American revenue is exposed to the more resilient Repair and Remodel (R&R) market, and 4) EBIT margins recently benefiting from structural lift due to new manufacturing processes.

Nathan Reilly on Incitec Pivot Ltd: We have a Buy rating on IPL. Explosives operating outlook is supportive of ongoing earnings growth, and we expect ongoing AN price recontracting benefits to support c.7% EBIT underlying growth across Explosives in both the Americas and APAC. With the close of the WALA transaction on 1 Dec 2023, IPL intends to return up to A\$1.0bn of proceeds from the sale to shareholders net proceeds A\$1.3bn. Due diligence is now complete for the pending Fertilisers process, with an update to be provided on separation plans “in due course”. Cashing out of Waggaman.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Australia Materials

Levi Spry on Northern Star Resources: We are Neutral on NST because 1) We like gold but it is starting to get valuation challenged, 2) In midst of capex cycle, and 3) Cautious about 2H weighting to production guidance and continued cost pressures in WA.

Nathan Reilly on Orora Ltd: We are Neutral on Orora. Recently completed the A\$2.2bn acquisition of Saverglass, a global manufacturer of glass bottles for the premium and ultra premium wine/spirits markets. At its recent trading update, Orora noted global market conditions are subdued as a result of customer destocking and softness in consumer demand, underpinning our forecasts for a 10% decline in Saverglass’s EBITDA (constant FX) in FY24 (vs. initial guidance for flat earnings). Can Saverglass deliver earnings growth in FY24?

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Real Estate

Grant McCasker on GPT group: We have a Buy rating on GPT. We see deep value in the quality diversified portfolio with sectors ex. office performing well and potential market focus form the new CEO’s strategy to come in 2024.

Grant McCasker on Homeco Daily Needs REIT: We have a Buy rating on the stock. We like HDN's embedded development opportunities, which provide superior growth vs. mid-cap mall peers. Growth should come from active portfolio management, developments, strong reversion on expiries (6% in FY24) and high fixed annual rent reviews.

We would like to thank Shiva Rohith Adhikari and Sonam Jagga, our research support service professionals, for their assistance in preparing this research report.

KEY DEFINITIONS: Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR) Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Short-Term Ratings reflect the expected near-term (up to three months) performance of the stock and do not reflect any change in the fundamental view or investment case Equity Price Targets have an investment horizon of 12 months.

EXCEPTIONS AND SPECIAL CASES: UK and European Investment Fund ratings and definitions are: Buy: Positive on factors such as structure, management, performance record, discount; Neutral: Neutral on factors such as structure, management, performance record, discount; Sell: Negative on factors such as structure, management, performance record, discount. Core Banding Exceptions (CBE): Exceptions to the standard +/-6% bands may be granted by the Investment Review Committee (IRC). Factors considered by the IRC include the stock's volatility and the credit spread of the respective company's debt. As a result, stocks deemed to be very high or low risk may be subject to higher or lower bands as they relate to the rating. When such exceptions apply, they will be identified in the Company Disclosures table in the relevant research piece.

Research analysts contributing to this report who are employed by any non-US affiliate of UBS Securities LLC are not registered/ qualified as research analysts with FINRA. Such analysts may not be associated persons of UBS Securities LLC and therefore are not subject to the FINRA restrictions on communications with a subject company, public appearances, and trading securities held by a research analyst account. ## Company profile and fee and risk statement under the Japanese Financial Instruments & Exchange Law

Company Name etc: UBS Securities Japan Co., Ltd., Financial Instruments & Exchange Firm, Kanto Local Financial Bureau (Kinsho) No.2633

Associated Memberships: Japan Securities Dealers' Association, the Financial Futures Association of Japan, and Type II Financial Instruments Firms Association

UBS Securities Japan Co., Ltd. will receive a brokerage fee based on an individual contract and no standard upper limit or calculating method. For the trading of domestic stocks, consumption tax is added to the fee. For the trading of foreign stock, fee on the foreign stock exchange or foreign tax may be charged in addition to the domestic fee and tax. Those amounts may vary depending on the jurisdiction. There is a risk that a loss may occur due to a change in the price of the stock in the case of trading stocks, and that a loss may occur due to the exchange rate in the case of trading foreign stocks. There is a risk that a loss may occur due to a change in the price or performance of the properties in the portfolio in the case of trading REITs

UBS Securities Japan Co., Ltd. will only receive the purchasing amounts for trading unlisted bonds (JGBs, municipals, government guaranteed bonds, corporate bonds) when UBS Securities Japan Co., Ltd. is the counterparty. There is a risk that a loss may occur due to a change in the price of the bond caused by the fluctuations in the interest rates, and that a loss may occur due to the exchange rate in the case of trading foreign bonds.

For the detailed information of commissions and risks associated with individual financial instrument transactions, please carefully read the documents provided prior to the execution of the contracts or other materials for customers, etc. before executing any transaction. If you need any clarification, please consult with our sales person in charge.
